Deferred & Reserved — Explicit Non-Drop Register

These items are intentionally out of the current release. Each is reserved behind an entitlement flag (see P5) or an infra seam so onboarding config, RBAC surface, and BI dashboards render them as "not enabled" rather than absent. This register is the single source of truth for "why isn't X here?".

  • D9 — ERPNext accounting / double-entry GL: AURA remains a receivables ledger (invoices/payments/receipts, server-side tax). GL, journals, chart-of-accounts, and financial statements are PHASE-2, exported via the reserved message-broker seam in lib/infra-seams.ts. See l7.
  • Payment gateway (card capture / online settlement): PHASE-2. Today money movement is ledger-only with idempotency_keys; no external PSP. See m2, P5.
  • DocuSign / qualified e-signature on finance (D4) and delivery (D6) packets: PHASE-2. PDFs are generated via pdfkit; signing is manual/out-of-band. See m4, m6.
  • Telematics / connected-vehicle (D5): PHASE-2. Inventory status is manual/import-driven. See m5, P5.
  • Freight-forwarder / shipping-line EDI (D6) and customer self-service service booking (D7): PHASE-2, reserved behind entitlement flags.
  • Infra guarantees (backups/DR, encryption-at-rest, uptime SLO, distributed tracing, message broker): INFRA-SEAM — present as no-op seams in lib/infra-seams.ts; contracts specified, implementations pending platform infra.

Cross-Module Tie-Ins Validated by This Matrix

The matrix deliberately shows the same REAL entities crossing domain boundaries, proving the modules are integrated rather than siloed:

  • vehicles/assets flow D5 Inventory → D2 Deal (VIN-lock, agent A11) → D6 Delivery (auto-delivery, PDI) — see m5, m2, m6.
  • customers/customer_personas span D1 Lead → D3 Appraisal → D2 Deal via persona/vehicle recommend — see m4, m3.
  • gates unify below-floor discount (D2) and every checklist/approval gate; single gates/:id/resolve path — see r2.
  • invoices/payments/receipts and dealer_taxes bind D2 Deal, D7 Service, D8 Parts → D9 Financial-mgmt — see m9, r5.
  • agent_runs/audit_logs/timeline_events make D10 Governance observe every write in every domain — dealer-scoped without exception (CORRECTION) — see l6, P6.
  • parts ↔ job_card_parts couple D8 Parts ↔ D7 Service at consumption — see m8, m7.

Validation V3 — System Invariants & Guarantees (the foolproof backbone)

This section is the machine-checkable contract for the word "correct" in AURA. Everything else in the spec describes what the system does; V3 states the invariants that must never be violated, where each is enforced (which layer/middleware/DB constraint), and how it is verified. An invariant that is only "enforced" in the UI is treated as not enforced — every guarantee below is anchored at the deepest layer that can be reached by an attacker or a bug (DB constraint > server middleware > handler > Zod contract > UI). The UI layer merely makes violations hard to attempt; the server layer makes them impossible to commit.

Invariant IDs are stable (INV-*) and are referenced from the module sections. Each invariant lists the REAL tables, endpoints, and agents it constrains so the tie-back to the rest of the spec is explicit (see R2 tenancy, R3 RBAC, R4 money/ledger, R5 AI governance, R6 state machines wherever those IDs appear).

V3.0 — The layering rule (how to read "where enforced")

Enforcement precedence, deepest wins. A guarantee is only as strong as its lowest listed layer:

LayerMechanismBypassable byRole in an invariant
L0 DatabasePostgreSQL NOT NULL, CHECK, UNIQUE, FK, partial indexes, tenant-column compositesNothing short of a migrationLast-resort truth; catches handler bugs and raw SQL
L1 Server middlewarerequireAuth, tenant resolver (activeDealerId+x-dealer-id), requirePermission(module,category), idempotency middleware, guardUntrusted, isAgentEnabledOnly a middleware-ordering bugPrimary enforcement for tenancy, RBAC, idempotency, AI governance
L2 Handler / serviceState-machine validators, gate checks, tax/GRA compute, ledger posting, recordAgentRunA code path that skips the serviceBusiness invariants that need row context
L3 Contract (Zod from OpenAPI)163-path OpenAPI → Zod request/response parseAny non-HTTP mutationShape/format validation (VIN, registration, enums)
L4 UI (react-query)Disabled buttons, single Advance CTA, field-permission maskingcurl, devtools, replayErgonomics only — never the sole enforcement of any invariant

Rule: if an invariant below lists only L4, it is a defect. Every invariant here lists at least one of L0–L2.

V3.1 — Tenant isolation (the strongest invariant)

INV-TEN-1 — Every data-plane read and write is dealer-scoped

  • Statement: No row belonging to dealer A is ever readable or mutable in a request whose resolved activeDealerId is B. This holds for every data-plane table (leads, customers, deals, quotes, invoices, payments, service_orders, job_cards, documents, agent_runs, activity, timeline_events, gates, notifications, tasks, whatsapp_conversations, conversations/messages, audit_logs) with no exception — including the Concierge (anthropic/conversations, copilotkit), object-storage ACL (storage/*, GCS presign), and /audit-logs (CORRECTION applied; these were the historical leak points).
  • Where enforced: L1 tenant resolver derives activeDealerId from the caller's dealer_users membership and requires header x-dealer-id to match a membership row (else the request never reaches a handler). L2 every Drizzle query is built through the scoped repository which injects where dealerId = activeDealerId; direct table access without the scope wrapper is banned by lint rule. L0 tenant-bearing tables carry dealerId NOT NULL + composite indexes so an unscoped query is both incorrect and slow (fails review).
  • Cross-tenant behavior (CORRECTION): a request that names a real id belonging to another dealer returns 404, not 403, at the data-plane — no existence leak. 403 is reserved for the case where membership itself is the check (e.g., x-dealer-id is a dealer the caller could belong to but does not, or an RBAC category is missing). See INV-TEN-2.
  • Division axis: where a second axis applies (CAM/GT via divisions / dealer_users.divisionId), the same wrapper adds the division predicate for division-scoped roles; GMs (isGeneralManager) see all divisions of their dealer.
  • How verified: (1) contract test per data-plane path issues a request with a valid session for dealer B and an id owned by dealer A → asserts 404 and empty body; (2) property test enumerates all repository methods and asserts each emitted SQL contains the dealerId predicate (AST check); (3) a "poison row" seeded in dealer A is asserted absent from every list/search/report/export/concierge answer run as dealer B; (4) storage test: presigned URL minted under dealer A is asserted un-mintable and the object un-listable under dealer B.

INV-TEN-2 — Header/session/membership must agree; 403 vs 404 is deterministic

  • Statement: x-dealer-id ∈ caller's dealer_users memberships, else 403. Given a valid active dealer, a resource id outside that dealer → 404. The two codes are never interchanged.
  • Where enforced: L1 resolver (403 branch) then L2 scoped repository (404 branch, because the scoped query simply returns no row).
  • How verified: matrix test over {no header, foreign-but-nonmember header, member header + foreign id, member header + own id} → {400, 403, 404, 200}.

INV-TEN-3 — Platform vs Dealer plane separation

  • Statement: Control-plane routes /platform/{dealers, dealers/:id/members, users} require super-admin (SUPER_ADMIN_EMAIL) and are the only way to create dealers/memberships; dealer-settings routes /admin/* require dealer settings:admin. A dealer-plane token can never reach a platform mutation and vice-versa. Users are GLOBAL (Clerk clerkId); role/membership is per-dealer in dealer_users. JIT provisioning on first login; the retired "first-user-becomes-GM" path must not exist.
  • Where enforced: L1 requireSuperAdmin on /platform/*; L1 requirePermission('settings','admin') on /admin/*. L0 dealers.createdBy + membership FKs.
  • How verified: a dealer-admin token hitting any /platform/* path asserts 403; regression test asserts no code path creates a dealer_users row with implicit GM.

V3.2 — Money idempotency & ledger integrity

INV-MON-1 — Every money-moving write is idempotent by Idempotency-Key

  • Statement: Replaying a create against payments, receipts, invoices, service_invoices, part_purchases (and any endpoint that posts to the ledger) with the same Idempotency-Key returns the original result and creates exactly one row — never a duplicate charge or receipt.
  • Where enforced: L1 idempotency middleware persists idempotency_keys(key, dealerId, requestHash, responseSnapshot, status) before the handler; a repeat key with a matching hash short-circuits to the stored response, a repeat key with a different body → 409. L0 UNIQUE(dealerId, key) guarantees single-writer even under a race.
  • How verified: concurrent-replay test fires N identical requests → asserts one row and identical responses; body-mismatch test asserts 409; the key is asserted dealer-scoped (INV-TEN-1) so keys never collide across tenants.

INV-MON-2 — Ledger, not gateway; balances derive from postings

  • Statement: AURA has no payment gateway — money is a ledger. Outstanding balance (outstanding-balances) is always a server-side aggregation of invoices − payments/receipts; it is never stored as a mutable scalar the client can set. No negative payment, no payment exceeding invoice remainder without an explicit over-payment/credit path.
  • Where enforced: L2 balance service computes on read; L0 CHECK(amount > 0) on payment/receipt rows; L2 remainder check before posting.
  • How verified: ledger reconciliation test asserts Σposts = reported balance for randomized histories; over-post attempt asserts rejection.

INV-MON-3 — Currency & scale are single-sourced

  • Statement: Money is stored USD-scale; GYD display = USD × dealer.usdExchangeRate (~209). Conversion happens only at the presentation boundary; no persisted amount is ever in GYD. Dates are stored UTC, displayed America/Guyana (GMT-4).
  • Where enforced: L2 formatter reads the tenant's rate from dealers.usdExchangeRate (INV-TEN-1 scoped); L3 response schema types amounts as USD-scale integers/decimals.
  • How verified: snapshot test asserts stored value invariant under display-rate change; boundary test at GMT-4 midnight asserts correct Guyana calendar day.

V3.3 — No irreversible action without a gate

INV-GATE-1 — Regulated / capital / credit / below-floor / VIN-committing actions require an approved gate

  • Statement: An action that is irreversible or externally binding cannot commit while an open gates row governs it. The gated set is first-class and exhaustive:
Gate triggerEndpoint(s)RuleResolution
Deal below floorPOST /deals, deal editDiscount > 5% of floor opens a below-floor gategates/:id/resolve by authorized role
VIN allocation / lockdeals VIN-lock, deliveries/advanceA VIN (vehicles/assets) binds to exactly one active deal (see INV-VIN-1); allocation via A11 is a gate, not an auto-commitHuman confirm; A11 is a suggestion only
Finance / credit submitfinance-applications/submit, los-submissionsSubmission to Demerara LOS is irreversible-external → gatedExplicit submit after documents complete
Regulated GRA filinggra/filingsSee INV-AI-6 — server-computed duty, human gate before filingHuman approve
Delivery commitdeliveries/advance (11-step)PDI + invoice must exist before delivery closesStep machine + gate
Customer-facing sendoutreach, quote send, notify-owner, whatsappHITL Approve&Send (INV-AI-4)Human Approve
  • Where enforced: L2 handlers call assertNoOpenGate(entity) before commit and open gates via the gates service; L0 partial unique index prevents two approved conflicting commits (e.g., VIN double-lock). Gate state itself is a state machine (INV-STATE-1).
  • How verified: for each row above, a test attempts the commit with an open gate → asserts 422/blocked; then resolves the gate → asserts commit succeeds and is audited (INV-AUD-1).

V3.4 — AI safety envelope (all agents, no exceptions)

Applies to every agent: A1 Intake/Dedup, A2 Round-robin (deterministic), A3 Quote/Code (deterministic tax + pdfkit), A5 Doc pre-fill (vision, HITL), A6 Call sentiment, A7 Outreach composer (HITL), A10 Test-drive availability, A11 VIN allocation, A15 Pipeline suggestions, Sentiment digest, Persona/vehicle recommend, Concierge (SSE + copilotkit, nav-only), GRA vision extract.

INV-AI-1 — Kill-switch: no AI call while disabled

  • Statement: Every agent invocation checks isAgentEnabled against agents.status for the tenant; a disabled agent makes zero model calls and the feature degrades gracefully (deterministic fallback or "unavailable"), never a hard error that blocks core flow.
  • Where enforced: L1/L2 isAgentEnabled guard wraps the single agent entrypoint; the Anthropic client is unreachable except through it. PATCH /agents that flips the switch is gated at governance level (CORRECTION), not ordinary edit.
  • How verified: disable each agent → assert 0 outbound model calls (client spy) and the fallback path returns; assert PATCH /agents requires the governance permission.

INV-AI-2 — Grounded output; no fabrication

  • Statement: Agents may only assert facts derivable from retrieved tenant data or the source document. Vision agents (A5, GRA extract) transcribe legible fields only and must not invent values; missing/illegible → explicit "unreadable", never a guess.
  • Where enforced: L2 prompt contracts + post-parse validators reject outputs referencing ids/entities not present in the scoped context; GRA duty is server-computed, AI never supplies the number (INV-AI-6).
  • How verified: adversarial fixtures (blurred docs, absent fields) assert "unreadable"; grounding test asserts every entity id in output exists in the request's scoped context.

INV-AI-3 — guardUntrusted on ALL untrusted text

  • Statement: Any text originating outside the trust boundary — inbound WhatsApp/Twilio/email bodies, webhook payloads (Meta Lead Ads), uploaded document OCR, customer notes, call transcripts — passes through guardUntrusted before it is placed in any prompt, so it cannot act as an instruction (prompt-injection containment). CORRECTION: this is all untrusted text, not a subset.
  • Where enforced: L1/L2 guardUntrusted at every ingestion seam (webhooks, IMAP intake, uploads) and again at prompt-assembly.
  • How verified: injection corpus ("ignore previous instructions…", tool-invocation strings) fed through each seam → assert wrapped/neutralized and that the model's behavior is unchanged.

INV-AI-4 — HITL before any customer-facing send

  • Statement: No AI-composed content reaches a customer without a human Approve&Send. Covers A7 outreach, quote send (quote/send, quote PDF), notify-owner, WhatsApp composs, doc pre-fill (A5 HITL). The Concierge is nav-only — it can navigate/read, never send or mutate on the customer's behalf.
  • Where enforced: L2 send handlers require an approval token/gate produced by an explicit human action; Concierge tool surface excludes any send/mutate tool.
  • How verified: attempt to send without approval → blocked; assert Concierge tool registry contains only read/navigate tools.

INV-AI-5 — Confidence floor + audited every run

  • Statement: An agent result below MIN_AGENT_CONFIDENCE is not auto-applied — it routes to human review (CORRECTION: enforce the floor, don't just record it). Every invocation, applied or not, writes agent_runs via recordAgentRun with inputs (PII-minimized), output, confidence, and decision.
  • Where enforced: L2 single agent entrypoint: recordAgentRun is unconditional; the confidence gate branches auto-apply vs review (agents/runs/:id/review).
  • How verified: low-confidence fixture asserts no auto-apply + a review task; assert every agent path produces an agent_runs row (no-run = defect); assert stored inputs are PII-minimized.

INV-AI-6 — GRA is server-computed, Guyana-specific, human-gated

  • Statement: Guyana Revenue Authority duty/tax is computed by the server from structured inputs; the AI (gra/extract) only extracts legible document fields. No AI-produced duty figure is ever filed. Filing (gra/filings) requires a human gate (INV-GATE-1).
  • Where enforced: L2 GRA compute service is deterministic; the extract path has no write access to the duty figure.
  • How verified: compute unit tests against GRA schedules; assert filing endpoint rejects any client-supplied duty value.

V3.5 — State transitions only via validated machines

INV-STATE-1 — No status is set by raw write; every transition is validated

  • Statement: Lifecycle fields (lead stage, deal status, delivery 11-step, service order, job card, finance application, gate, booking) change only through their transition service, which validates the source→target edge and preconditions. Direct UPDATE status=… is forbidden.
  • Where enforced: L2 machine per entity; L0 optional CHECK on enum + (where practical) transition-audit trigger. Lead stage advance is the canonical case: POST /leads/:id/advance checks the versioned stage_checklists and returns 422 {unmet:[…]} when preconditions fail — the only way to move a stage.
  • Where tied: approvals are the gates entity (INV-GATE-1); delivery advance shares the machine pattern (deliveries/advance).
  • How verified: exhaustive edge test per machine asserts illegal edges rejected and legal edges audited; advance test asserts 422 body lists exactly the unmet checklist items for the entity's checklist version.

INV-STATE-2 — Single Advance CTA; Review disambiguated

  • Statement: There is exactly one "Advance" affordance per entity, bound to the transition service (CORRECTION: one Advance CTA). "Review" is a first-class, disambiguated concept — agents/runs/:id/review (agent-output review), lead review, and gate resolution are distinct entities/actions, not one overloaded label. Case, Test-Drive, and Review are first-class entities.
  • Where enforced: L4 renders a single Advance CTA (ergonomic) but L2 remains the sole committer; naming enforced by the contract's operation ids.
  • How verified: UI test asserts one Advance control per entity view; contract test asserts the three review surfaces have distinct operation ids and payloads.

V3.6 — Format & shape validation (VIN / registration / 17-char)

INV-FMT-1 — VIN and engineNumber are exactly 17 chars; registration matches pattern

  • Statement: vehicles/assets VIN and engineNumber are 17 characters; vehicle registration matches ^[A-Z]{3}[0-9]{1,4}$. Enforced on create, import (vehicles/import, template), and edit.
  • Where enforced: L3 Zod schema (17-char + regex) rejects at the contract boundary for every path incl. bulk import; L0 CHECK(char_length(vin)=17) and a registration format check as backstop; L2 import validates row-by-row and reports rejects rather than partially committing.
  • How verified: boundary tests (16/17/18 chars, lowercase, bad registration) assert rejection at L3 and L0; import test with mixed valid/invalid rows asserts all-or-reported behavior, no half-import.

INV-VIN-1 — A VIN binds to at most one active deal/delivery

  • Statement: Once VIN-locked to a deal, the same VIN cannot be locked to another active deal (prevents double-sell).
  • Where enforced: L0 partial unique index on (dealerId, vin) where deal active; L2 VIN-lock service checks + A11 only suggests (INV-GATE-1).
  • How verified: concurrent lock test asserts exactly one winner, the other 409/blocked.

V3.7 — Documents & object storage are dealer-ACL'd

  • INV-DOC-1 statement: Every object in documents/customer_documents/finance_documents and its GCS blob is dealer-scoped; presigned URLs are minted only for objects owned by the caller's active dealer and expire; no cross-tenant listing or fetch (CORRECTION: object-storage ACL is dealer-scoped like every other query — this was a historical gap).
  • Where enforced: L1/L2 storage service checks ownership before presign (storage/*); L0 dealerId on document rows + object key namespaced by dealer.
  • How verified: presign-as-B for A's object → 404; list-as-B → A's docs absent; expired-URL test asserts denial after TTL.

V3.8 — Everything is audited; impersonation is audited, not bypassed

  • INV-AUD-1 statement: Every mutation and every privileged read writes audit_logs (and where relevant activity/timeline_events) with actor (Clerk clerkId), dealer, entity, before/after, and correlation id. audit_logs is itself dealer-scoped (INV-TEN-1) and append-only.
  • Impersonation (CORRECTION): support/impersonation is a first-class audited action — the raw AUTH_BYPASS / x-test-user-email backdoors are retired. Impersonation records who impersonated whom, in which dealer, for how long.
  • Where enforced: L1 audit middleware on mutating verbs + L2 explicit audit for reads of sensitive entities; L0 append-only (no UPDATE/DELETE grant on audit table to app role).
  • How verified: every mutating endpoint asserts an audit row with actor+diff; assert no code references the retired bypass env vars; impersonation test asserts a paired start/stop audit entry.

V3.9 — Reports & aggregates are server-side

  • INV-RPT-1 statement: reports, dashboard/*, agents/metrics, and outstanding-balances aggregate on the server over dealer-scoped data (CORRECTION); the client never sums authoritative figures and can never widen scope past its tenant/division.
  • Where enforced: L2 aggregation services built on the scoped repository (INV-TEN-1); L3 response schemas return computed totals, not raw cross-tenant rows.
  • How verified: report-as-B excludes A's poison row; division-scoped role sees only its division's totals; assert totals equal server recomputation.

V3.10 — Governance & infra seams

  • INV-GOV-1: Agent enable/disable and entitlements/feature-flags are per-dealer and changed only through governance-gated endpoints (PATCH /agents, /admin/*). PENDING-INFRA capabilities (backups/DR, encryption-at-rest, uptime, tracing, message broker) are explicit no-op seams in lib/infra-seams.ts — they must fail closed and be clearly marked, never silently assumed present.
  • How verified: assert the governance permission is required to flip any agent/flag; assert infra seams are referenced through the single seam module so production wiring is a one-file change.

V3.11 — Invariant → verification traceability matrix

Definition of "correct": a change to AURA is correct only if it preserves every invariant above under the listed verification. A red test on any row is a release blocker. New endpoints inherit these invariants by construction — the scoped repository, idempotency middleware, transition services, guardUntrusted/recordAgentRun/isAgentEnabled wrappers, and audit middleware are the shared machinery, so "forgot to enforce it" is designed out rather than reviewed for.

Validation V4 — Open Items & Honest Limits

Everything specified in R1–R3 and the platform/tenancy layers is implementable, testable, and foolproof within the boundaries drawn here. This section is the deliberate, honest inventory of what AURA cannot close on its own — because closure depends on either a client policy decision, a third-party contract/credential, or an infrastructure procurement (the PENDING-INFRA seams in lib/infra-seams.ts). None of these are code defects; each is a decision or a dependency. For every open item we state the real code seam it lands on, the runtime behavior today, the impact if left open, and who must decide.

AURA is 100% foolproof within these boundaries; these items ARE the boundary. Nothing below is a bug — each is a documented seam awaiting a decision or a credential.

V4.1 — How to read this section

  • Seam — the concrete table, endpoint, or lib/infra-seams.ts no-op the item resolves against. Nothing is hand-wavy; every open item has an address in the real system.
  • Today — the deterministic, safe behavior the system exhibits right now (a no-op, a ledger-only path, a human gate, a conservative default). No open item degrades safety; each degrades only to a documented, auditable holding pattern.
  • Decision owner — the single role accountable for closing the item. Where a Super-Admin platform action is involved it ties to /platform/* and /admin/* governance (see the Platform Console layer).
  • Foolproofing today — the guardrail that prevents the open item from causing silent data corruption, money errors, or a trust breach while it remains open.

V4.2 — Category A: Third-party integrations awaiting contract/credentials

A1 — Real payment gateway + refund reconciliation

  • Seam: payments, receipts, invoices, outstanding-balances, idempotency_keys; endpoints POST /payments, POST /receipts, GET /outstanding-balances. Confirmed system fact: NO payment gateway — ledger only.
  • Today: AURA is a ledger of record. Payments are recorded against invoices as money-movement facts (USD-scale storage, GYD display via dealer.usdExchangeRate). Every money-mutating write is protected by idempotency_keys so a retried request never double-posts. There is no card capture, no gateway settlement callback, and therefore no automated refund: a refund today is a manually-recorded negative/offsetting payments row with an audit trail.
  • Impact if left open: Reconciliation between AURA's ledger and the bank/gateway statement is a manual back-office step. Refund reconciliation (matching an outbound refund to its original inbound payment) has no automated three-way match. No customer-facing "pay now" surface exists.
  • Decision owner: Client finance leadership (gateway selection + merchant account) plus dealer settings:admin for tax/receipt config. Guyana-market gateway availability must be confirmed by the client; there is no Guyanese card gateway assumed.
  • Foolproofing today: idempotency on all money writes; outstanding-balances computed server-side (never client-trusted, see reports correction); receipts are immutable once issued; the below-floor deal gate (>5% off floor → gates, see R2) still governs discounts regardless of how money is later collected.

A2 — Real GRA e-filing integration

  • Seam: gra/extract, gra/filings, GRA vision-extract agent, gates, documents.
  • Today: GRA is Guyana and is treated per the correction: duty/tax is server-computed, the AI vision agent only extracts legible fields from uploaded documents (no fabrication), and a human gate (gates + HITL review) confirms extraction before any filing artifact is produced. Filings today are prepared/exported artifacts, not transmitted to a live GRA endpoint.
  • Impact if left open: Filing submission to the Guyana Revenue Authority is manual/out-of-band — AURA prepares the computed figures and extracted evidence, a human files them. No automated filing acknowledgement is ingested back.
  • Decision owner: Client + GRA — availability of an official GRA e-filing API and credentials is a government/regulatory dependency outside AURA's control. Client tax/compliance lead owns the duty parameters (see A4/B2).
  • Foolproofing today: AI is confidence-gated (MIN_AGENT_CONFIDENCE enforced) and guardUntrusted wraps all extracted document text; the server computes duty (AI never computes money); every extraction run is audited via recordAgentRun; a human gate is mandatory before a filing artifact is trusted. No number reaches a filing without server computation + human confirmation.

A3 — DocuSign / e-signature (Phase 2)

  • Seam: documents, customer_documents, deliveries (11-step advance, PDI, delivery.invoice.pdf), quotes (quote PDF), finance_documents.
  • Today: Documents are generated (pdfkit), stored (GCS presigned), and human-signed out of band. There is no embedded e-signature ceremony and no signature-event webhook.
  • Impact if left open: Signature capture on quotes, delivery acceptance, and finance packets is a wet/scanned-signature workflow; the signed PDF is re-uploaded as a document. No automated "all parties signed" state transition.
  • Decision owner: Client (Phase 2 scope + DocuSign account). Explicitly deferred to Phase 2.
  • Foolproofing today: the delivery 11-step advance and the single Advance CTA (see corrections + R3) still enforce that a delivery cannot complete without the required document artifacts present; document presence is checked server-side against the versioned checklist.

A4 — ERPNext accounting sync (Phase 2)

  • Seam: invoices, payments, receipts, dealer_taxes, service_invoices; message-broker seam in lib/infra-seams.ts (PENDING-INFRA).
  • Today: AURA is the authoritative ledger; there is no outbound accounting sync. The broker seam is a no-op, so no financial events are published to an external ERP.
  • Impact if left open: GL posting into the client's accounting system is manual export/import. No real-time subledger→GL reconciliation.
  • Decision owner: Client finance (Phase 2 scope + ERPNext instance/credentials).
  • Foolproofing today: money is dealer-scoped and division-tagged (CAM/GT), USD-scale with a single conversion source (dealer.usdExchangeRate), so the eventual export has a clean, unambiguous, tenant-partitioned source of truth. The broker seam is designed as a real integration point, not a rewrite.

A5 — Mileage / telematics integration

  • Seam: vehicles, assets, service_orders, coverage_plans, bookings (service reminders).
  • Today: Mileage is human-entered at service intake/job-card creation. There is no telematics feed; service-due and coverage-expiry reminders (service-orders/remind, coverage/remind, bookings/remind) fire on time-based rules and last-recorded odometer.
  • Impact if left open: No automatic mileage-triggered service reminders; usage-based warranty/coverage checks rely on the odometer captured at the last visit.
  • Decision owner: Client — whether a telematics provider is in scope and which one (vehicle-brand OEM feed vs aftermarket device). Depends on B3 warranty terms to know which mileage thresholds matter.
  • Foolproofing today: mileage is validated as a monotonic, non-negative integer at entry; coverage-plan evaluation is server-side; reminders are idempotent and dealer-scoped.

V4.3 — Category B: Client policy decisions (config, not code)

These items are fully implemented as parameterized, versioned configuration — the code path exists and is enforced; only the values await client sign-off. Each maps to a real /admin/* settings surface or a versioned checklist, so closing them is a data decision, not a deployment.

B1 — Service SLA window (24h vs 7d)

  • Seam: service_orders, job_cards, stage_checklists (versioned), service-orders/remind.
  • Today: the SLA breach clock and reminder cadence are driven by a configurable window. The system needs the client to fix whether the standard service-completion SLA is 24 hours or 7 days (or per-service-type).
  • Impact if left open: breach flags, escalation timing, and reminder frequency are running on a placeholder default; dashboards/reports SLA metrics reflect that default.
  • Decision owner: Dealer service management (CAM Motors / GT Automotive) via settings:admin. May differ per division.
  • Foolproofing today: SLA is a versioned parameter — changing it does not retro-rewrite closed orders; each order records the SLA version in effect at creation.

B2 — Duty-free threshold + EV exclusions

  • Seam: dealer_taxes, admin/taxes, server-side duty computation (see A2), quotes, deals.
  • Today: duty is server-computed from a tax table; the specific Guyana duty-free thresholds and whether/which EV categories are excluded or concessioned must be confirmed against current GRA policy.
  • Impact if left open: quoted landed cost and duty on affected vehicle classes may use provisional rates; EVs specifically may be over- or under-charged duty until the concession rule is confirmed.
  • Decision owner: Client tax/compliance lead, validated against current GRA schedules (regulatory source of truth).
  • Foolproofing today: AI never computes duty (A2 correction); rates live in a single versioned dealer_taxes source; a quote records the tax version used so figures are reproducible and auditable.

B3 — Warranty types / terms

  • Seam: coverage_plans, coverage/remind, service_orders, assets, deliveries.
  • Today: coverage plans are modeled generically (duration + mileage cap + inclusions). The concrete warranty product catalog — manufacturer vs dealer warranty, extended-warranty tiers, powertrain vs bumper-to-bumper, transferability — awaits the client's warranty policy.
  • Impact if left open: coverage validity checks at service intake run against placeholder plan definitions; expiry reminders use provisional terms.
  • Decision owner: Client (OEM/dealer warranty policy). Interacts with A5 (mileage source) and B1 (service SLA).
  • Foolproofing today: coverage evaluation is server-side and plan-versioned; a delivered vehicle's coverage is snapshotted at delivery so later catalog edits don't silently alter an existing customer's entitlement.

B4 — Discount-approval matrix

  • Seam: deals (below-floor gate at >5%), gates (+gates/resolve), approvals semantics, role_permissions, dealer_users.isGeneralManager/reportingManagerUserId.
  • Today: a discount beyond the floor (currently >5% off floor) opens a gate requiring approval. The tiered matrix — which discount band routes to sales manager vs GM vs platform, and the exact band boundaries — needs client sign-off.
  • Impact if left open: all above-threshold discounts route to a single approval tier rather than an escalating matrix; approval authority is coarser than the client may want.
  • Decision owner: Dealer sales leadership + GM via settings:admin and role configuration; escalation uses the existing reportingManagerUserId chain.
  • Foolproofing today: no discount past floor can be applied without a resolved gate; the gate + resolution are audited; the deal cannot advance (single Advance CTA, R2) with an unresolved gate → 422 {unmet:[]}.

B5 — Field-level persona access matrix

  • Seam: customer_personas, persona/recommend, role_field_permissions (hidden|view|edit), customers/persona.
  • Today: field-level RBAC is fully implemented (role_field_permissions); which specific persona fields each role may see/edit (e.g., income band, sensitivity flags, propensity scores) awaits the client's data-access matrix.
  • Impact if left open: persona fields are governed by a conservative default (restricted) until the client supplies the matrix; some roles may see less than intended, never more.
  • Decision owner: Client data-governance / privacy owner, applied via /admin/roles/:id/field-permissions.
  • Foolproofing today: default-deny on sensitive persona fields; persona is dealer-scoped; the recommend agent is PII-minimized and confidence-gated (MIN_AGENT_CONFIDENCE, guardUntrusted, recordAgentRun). Erring toward less exposure is the safe default.

V4.4 — Category C: PENDING-INFRA seams (procurement / platform)

These are the intentional no-op seams in lib/infra-seams.ts. Each is a real, named function boundary with a safe pass-through today, so wiring the eventual provider is a configuration/adapter task — not an architectural change. All are Super-Admin / platform concerns, governed by /platform/* and surfaced in the Platform Console.

Infra seam Behavior today (no-op) Impact if left open Decision owner
Backups / DR Seam present, no scheduled snapshot or cross-region replica wired. PostgreSQL runs single-region. No automated point-in-time recovery or disaster failover; RPO/RTO undefined. Client + hosting/platform ops (procurement).
Encryption-at-rest Transport (TLS) in place; at-rest KMS envelope encryption seam is a no-op. At-rest data protection relies on host defaults, not a managed KMS key policy. Client security/compliance + platform ops.
Uptime / HA Single-instance seam; no health-driven auto-failover or multi-AZ. No formal uptime SLA; maintenance windows cause downtime. Client (SLA target) + platform ops.
Distributed tracing Structured audit logging (audit_logs, agent_runs) is live; cross-service trace propagation seam is a no-op. Deep latency root-cause across services is manual; per-request spans not exported to an APM. Platform ops (APM tool selection).
Message broker Seam present; today integration/notification flows run in-process (email outbox retry/backoff/dedupe exists independently). No durable async event bus for ERP sync (A4), webhook fan-out, or high-volume decoupling. Platform ops + client (Phase 2 scope, ties to A4).

Note: the audit correction still holds even with tracing pending — every dealer-scoped query, concierge turn, audit-log read, and object-storage ACL check is tenant-partitioned today. The pending tracing seam concerns performance observability, not the security/audit posture, which is already complete (audit_logs, agent_runs, audited impersonation replacing raw AUTH_BYPASS/x-test-user-email).

V4.6 — What is NOT open (so the boundary is unambiguous)

To keep the honest limits honest, the following are closed and enforced today, and are explicitly out of this register:

  • Multi-tenancy isolation — every data-plane query dealer-scoped via activeDealerId(res) + x-dealer-id; cross-tenant read → 404 (no existence leak) at the data-plane, 403 only where membership itself is the check. Concierge, audit-logs, and object-storage ACL are all in scope (corrections applied).
  • Money integrity — USD-scale storage, single conversion source, idempotent money writes, server-computed outstanding-balances, below-floor gate. (Only collection/refund automation A1 and GL sync A4 are open.)
  • AI governance — isAgentEnabled kill-switch, recordAgentRun audit, PII-minimization, guardUntrusted on all untrusted text, enforced MIN_AGENT_CONFIDENCE, PATCH /agents gated at governance level. No AI fabricates money or duty (A2/B2).
  • Workflow correctness — single Advance CTA, stage advance 422 {unmet:[]} against versioned stage_checklists, first-class Case/Test-Drive/Review entities, disambiguated Review, server-side report aggregation.
  • Identity & RBAC — global Clerk users, per-dealer dealer_users membership, JIT provisioning, retired first-user-becomes-GM, module×category + field-level permissions, audited impersonation (raw AUTH_BYPASS/x-test-user-email retired).

AURA is 100% foolproof within these boundaries. The fifteen items in V4.5 are the boundary — each is a client decision or an infrastructure/credential dependency landing on a real, named seam, not a code defect. Closing any of them is a configuration, contract, or procurement action, not a re-architecture.
